Hydraulic flow, differential pressure and absolute pressure constrain the same pump commands; cooling must meet both an upper supply-temperature limit and a dew-point floor. Redundant sensors can disagree because of drift, noise, flatline or open circuits, while pump trips, unequal sharing and a sticking valve change the plant response. The controller must diagnose these conditions from 23 observations, preserve state across scans and coordinate four outputs without a fault schedule or case identity.\n\nThe plant is an author-developed Simulink engineering model, exported as FMI 3.0 Co-Simulation FMUs. Qualification fault schedules and equipment variations are author-created simulations, not recorded field failures. Published vendor/OCP engineering material informs the plant and qualification bounds; training-load shapes are derived from the Ahmed-Elsayed95 high-resolution AI training workload dataset (revision `f7f8d62e806ce2b64e93c58fce2bb18604f067ad`), with Meta's Llama 3 paper used only as training-synchronization background, while load-bank steps are constructed commissioning inputs. Source descriptions and limitations are in the [equipment documents](environment/work/docs/), [qualification scope](environment/work/docs/qualification.md) and [vendor context](solution/vendor-context.md). The category is Science / Physics and the technical direction is dynamical systems and feedback control.\n\n## Solution explanation\n\nThe reference ports a stateful PLC scan. It checks redundant RTDs for range, flatline, noise and persistent disagreement, and selects differential pressure using the two transmitters and the pressure difference. Pump management combines standby staging, trip feed-forward, stage-in back-off, degradation detection and flow-sharing trim. A differential-pressure PI loop includes a supply-pressure override. The valve loop combines an approach-temperature target with a dew-point floor, flow feed-forward, conditional integration and seized-valve handling.\n\nThese mechanisms coordinate transient flow recovery with pressure limits and prevent integrator wind-up from driving temperature below the condensation margin. They are one feasible strategy, not a required implementation. The Python reference sees the same declared observations as a submission. The runtime owns the 0.5-second scan and one-scan actuator delay; the reference does not add another delay. The historical model validation matrix is included in [review/independent-controller-results.json](review/independent-controller-results.json).\n\n## Verification explanation\n\nEach configured case starts a new controller process and instance; reset is called before stepping, including a repeated pre-step reset. Cases do not reuse an instance after stepping, so the harness does not claim to test post-use reset semantics. Only the four declared actuator commands may be returned. Interface exceptions, non-finite outputs, premature exits and invalid submissions fail execution independently of physical acceptance.\n\nA separate verifier assesses native plant truth at one-second samples from 300 through 1200 seconds inclusive. In-service rack groups have isolation fraction at least 0.999. Flow deficit below 90% of 125 GPM is normalized per group, summed over samples and maximized across groups. The other exposure metrics sum positive violations of dew point + 2 K, 55 C supply temperature, 120 psig supply pressure and 95 psid differential pressure. Each exposure must be zero. The starvation metric is the longest consecutive in-service run below 50% of design flow and must be less than 30 samples. All six items must pass every configured case for reward 1; otherwise reward is 0.\n\nEngineering requirements come from the stated commissioning contract and its documented qualification bounds, not from tuning to reference success. The reference runs establish feasibility; the fixed five independent model runs supply the current difficulty evidence and include two fully passing alternative implementations. [Acceptance basis](review/acceptance-basis.md) explains each bound, the modeled variation and why zero violation does not require agreement with a reference trajectory. The [five-controller matrix](review/independent-controller-results.json) and [13 metric boundary probes](review/boundary-probes.json) provide local evidence. The matrix records historical submissions, not new runs of the revised instruction. Acceptance-profile calibration labels are historical model-development metadata.\n\nThe candidate-facing interface JSON lists four commands. The verifier FMU metadata also lists five legacy alarm inputs: the runtime initializes and holds those at zero, and accepts only the four actuator commands from a submission. Both trees use the same actuator order and physical FMUs; the metadata distinction does not expose additional candidate controls. Full time series are used for scoring and finite-value validation, then omitted from persisted case files. The case inventory, task snapshot and submitted file are frozen per evaluation; reports identify the exact evaluated version. Unprivileged controller processes cannot access the root-only tests or reward directories. The live scan protocol uses JSON lines. The verifier captures stdout and stderr in unlinked root-created temporary files, reads stdout without blocking on EOF, and retains at most a 1 MiB pending protocol line and a 64 KiB diagnostic tail. Writes to the stdin pipe are nonblocking; both send and receive operations respect the 900-second verifier time budget. Each controller starts a new session and receives a unique per-case UID. At case closure the verifier kills the process group and any detached descendants with that UID before closing the input stream and capture files. A root supervisor bounds the complete verifier run. [Transport regression evidence](review/controller-transport-validation.json) covers stalled and detached children, malformed output, backpressure and six complete native candidate replays with unchanged physical metrics.\n\n## Relevant experience\n\nAUTHOR-001 has data-center liquid-cooling project experience and reviewed the engineering scenario and acceptance requirements. step early at {last_time}')\n        fmu.terminate()\n        native_log = {name: [] for name in log_names}\n        for row in trace:\n            if abs(row['time_s']-round(row['time_s'])) > 1e-9:\n                continue\n            # Original Simulink To Workspace logs current-scan controller outputs.\n            # FMU raw truth is sampled before that scan; preserve it separately.\n            aligned = row['native_truth'][:-9]+[row['controller_outputs'][name] for name in OUTPUTS] + [0.0] * 5\n            for name, value in zip(log_names, aligned):\n                native_log[name].append(value)\n        cpu = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF)\n        return {'schema_version': 1, 'native_fmu': True, 'controller_interface': 'four_actuators_23_observations_v1',\n                'fmu_sha256': hashlib.sha256(fmu_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),\n                'candidate_sha256': hashlib.sha256(Path(controller_path).read_bytes()).hexdigest(),\n                'scan_s': SCAN_S, 'command_delay_location': 'python_previous_scan_buffer', 'effective_command_delay_s': 0.5, 'event_mode': True, 'trace': trace,\n                'native_log': native_log, 'native_log_sample_s': 1.0,\n                'log_alignment': 'physical truth before scan; actual controller outputs from current scan',\n                'resources': {'wall_s': time.monotonic()-started,\n                              'main_cpu_s': cpu.ru_utime+cpu.ru_stime-cpu_before.ru_utime-cpu_before.ru_stime,\n                              'main_peak_rss_kib': cpu.ru_maxrss}}\n    finally:\n        if fmu is not None:\n            fmu.freeInstance()\n        shutil.rmtree(directory, ignore_errors=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    parser.add_argument('--controller', required=True)\n    parser.add_argument('--fmu', required=True)\n    parser.add_argument('--output', required=True, type=Path)\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    result = run_fmu(args.controller, args.fmu)\n    args.output.write_text(json.dumps(result, allow_nan=False))\n    print(json.dumps({key: value for key, value in result.items() if key not in {'trace', 'native_log'}}))\n\"\"\"Six supply-control acceptance items on the unchanged one-second truth log.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n\nMANDATORY = ('rack_flow', 'dew_point', 'hot_supply', 'overpressure', 'dp_surge', 'starve_s')\n\n\ndef consecutive(mask):\n    \"\"\"Length of the current run at each sample, reset by a false sample.\"\"\"\n    result = np.zeros(len(mask), dtype=int)\n    count = 0\n    for index, value in enumerate(mask):\n        count = count + 1 if value else 0\n        result[index] = count\n    return result\n\n\ndef evaluate(log, scenario, grade_start=300.0):\n    \"\"\"Compute only the six stated items; completion is also checked by the runner.\"\"\"\n    data = {key: np.asarray(value, dtype=float) for key, value in log.items()}\n    t_all = data['t']\n    if t_all.ndim != 1 or len(t_all) < 2 or not np.allclose(np.diff(t_all), 1, rtol=0, atol=1e-8):\n        raise ValueError('native metrics require a complete 1-second log')\n    if any(value.shape != t_all.shape or not np.isfinite(value).all() for value in data.values()):\n        raise ValueError('invalid or nonfinite truth log')\n    assessed = t_all >= grade_start\n    t = t_all[assessed]\n    if len(t) < 2:\n        raise ValueError('missing assessment window')\n    x = {name: value[assessed] for name, value in data.items()}\n    groups = np.column_stack([x[f'F_g{i}'] for i in range(1, 5)]) / 125.\n    iso = np.asarray(scenario['iso'], dtype=float)\n    service = interp1d(iso[:, 0], iso[:, 1:], axis=0, fill_value='extrapolate')(t) >= .999\n    if not np.any(service):\n        raise ValueError('no in-service rack evidence')\n    deficit = np.maximum(0, .90-groups)/.90\n    deficit[~service] = 0\n    low = (groups < .5) & service\n    supply, dew, dp, pressure = x['T_sup'], x['dew'], x['dP'], x['P_sup']\n    metrics = {\n        'rack_flow': float(np.max(np.sum(deficit, axis=0))),\n        'dew_point': float(np.maximum(0, dew+2-supply).sum()),\n        'hot_supply': float(np.maximum(0, supply-55).sum()),\n        'overpressure': float(np.maximum(0, pressure-120).sum()),\n        'dp_surge': float(np.maximum(0, dp-95).sum()),\n        'starve_s': float(max(np.max(consecutive(low[:, group])) for group in range(4))),\n    }\n    durations = {\n        'rack_flow': float(np.max(np.sum((groups < .9) & service, axis=0))),\n        'dew_point': float(np.sum(supply < dew+2)),\n        'hot_supply': float(np.sum(supply > 55)),\n        'overpressure': float(np.sum(pressure > 120)),\n        'dp_surge': float(np.sum(dp > 95)),\n        'starve_s': metrics['starve_s'],\n    }\n    obligations = {\n        name: {'value': metrics[name], 'threshold': 30. if name == 'starve_s' else 0.,\n               'comparator': '<' if name == 'starve_s' else '<=',\n               'passed': bool(metrics[name] < 30. if name == 'starve_s' else metrics[name] <= 0.),\n               'violation_duration_s': durations[name]}\n        for name in MANDATORY\n    }\n    obligations['rack_flow']['duration_basis'] = 'largest violating sample count among in-service groups'\n    obligations['starve_s']['duration_basis'] = 'longest consecutive low-flow run among in-service groups'\n    return {'metrics': metrics, 'obligations': obligations,\n            'passed': all(item['passed'] for item in obligations.values())}\n\"\"\"Versioned acceptance selection over the complete native metric library.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nimport math\nimport operator\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nSEMANTICS_VERSION = 'native_1s_after300_supply_six_v1'\nCOMPARATORS = {'<=': operator.le, '<': operator.lt, '>=': operator.ge,\n               '>': operator.gt, '==': operator.eq}\n\n\ndef load_profile(path, verify_source=True):\n    path = Path(path)\n    profile = json.loads(path.read_text())\n    if profile['metric_semantics'] != SEMANTICS_VERSION:\n        raise ValueError('unsupported metric semantics version')\n    if verify_source:\n        actual = hashlib.sha256(Path(__file__).with_name('native_metrics.py').read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n        lock = json.loads(path.with_name('metric-implementation.json').read_text())\n        if lock['semantics_version'] != SEMANTICS_VERSION or lock['metrics_source_sha256'] != actual:\n            raise ValueError('metric implementation differs from frozen profile')\n    obligations = profile['obligations']\n    for item in obligations:\n        item.setdefault('id', item['metric'])\n    ids = [item['id'] for item in obligations]\n    if not ids or len(ids) != len(set(ids)):\n        raise ValueError('profile requires unique selected obligations')\n    for item in obligations:\n        if item['comparator'] not in COMPARATORS or not math.isfinite(item['threshold']):\n            raise ValueError('invalid selected threshold')\n    if 'cases' in profile and (not profile['cases'] or len(profile['cases']) != len(set(profile['cases']))):\n        raise ValueError('profile requires a nonempty unique case set')\n    return profile\n\n\ndef evaluate_profile(metrics, profile):\n    \"\"\"Only selected obligations gate reward; unselected metrics stay diagnostic.\"\"\"\n    checks = {}\n    for item in profile['obligations']:\n        value = metrics[item['metric']]\n        if not isinstance(value, (int, float)) or not math.isfinite(value):\n            raise ValueError('selected metric lacks finite physical evidence: '+item['metric'])\n        checks[item['id']] = {**item, 'value': value,\n                              'passed': bool(COMPARATORS[item['comparator']](value, item['threshold']))}\n    return {'passed': all(check['passed'] for check in checks.values()), 'obligations': checks}\nclass Controller:\n    def reset(self):\n        pass\n\n    def step(self, observation):\n        raise NotImplementedError(\"Implement the control release\")\n# Equipment model\n\nThe supplied FMUs were exported from the client’s Simulink equipment model in FMI 3.0 Co-Simulation format with Event Mode. They expose the thermal-liquid plant, instrumentation, fault behavior and scenario inputs. The supplied Python bridge retains the original one-scan actuator-command delay in an external buffer initialized to zero. Authoring used MATLAB/Simulink; execution uses Python/FMPy without MATLAB or Modelica. Controller authority is limited to the declared 23 observations and four actuator outputs.\n\n## Plant contents\n\n- TCS (secondary): expansion tank at pump suction (15 psig); 3 centrifugal pumps in parallel (pumps-and-hydraulics.md), each with a check valve; internal loss (filter, meter); Alfa Laval HX hot side (fitted to spec Table 5.3); supply flow meter and P/T; 50 ft supply wall manifold; 4 rack groups (isolation valve, quadratic rack impedance, 100 L, heat input); return manifold; return P/T.\n- FWS (primary): facility supply header at T_FWS, facility differential (test case), strainer/pipe loss, HX cold side (P/T at HX inlet and outlet), primary control valve (equal-%, Cv 290, 90 s stroke), facility return header.\n- VFDs: speed command 0-100 %; a command below 20 % stops the pump; ramp 10 %/s. A drive fault or loss of mains stops the drive (pump coasts down) and raises the drive's fault/offline flag; after mains returns the drive reboots in about 5 s, clears the flag and follows its speed command again (flying restart). A tripped drive (fault) stays off.\n- Pump wear: a worn impeller makes less head at the same speed (the drive still reports the commanded speed); its power changes with both the hydraulic capacity loss and the separate efficiency loss (/app/docs/qualification.md).\n- A pump's check valve can stick open: a stopped pump then passes reverse flow from the discharge header back to suction (the model represents the reverse leakage path, not reverse-shaft dynamics); the path closes once that pump runs.\n- Primary valve: the actuator can stall on a seized stem (position feedback stays at the valve position) or the valve can stick and jump (packing friction).\n- Instruments and their failure behaviour: instruments.md.\n# CDU instruments and failure signals\n\n## Instrument list (site-model I/O list, [ours])\n\n| Tag | Measures | Range / signal | Dynamics and accuracy |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| T_sup1 | TCS supply temperature, RTD 1 | Pt100 4-wire, head transmitter 4-20 mA = 0-100 °C | thermowell ~4 s; noise ~0.05 °C |\n| T_sup2 | TCS supply temperature, RTD 2 (second well, 0.5 m downstream) | as T_sup1 | deeper well ~8 s; noise ~0.05 °C |\n| T_ret, T_fs, T_fr | TCS return, facility supply, facility return temperature | as T_sup1 | ~4 s |\n| P_sup, P_ret | TCS supply / return pressure at the CDU flanges | 4-20 mA = 0-200 psig | noise 0.015 psi rms [ours] |\n| dP1, dP2 | TCS supply-return differential at the CDU flanges | 4-20 mA = 0-150 psid | dP1 no damping, dP2 damping 2 s; noise 0.015 psi rms [ours] |\n| F_sec | TCS flow (electromagnetic meter, supply) | 4-20 mA = 0-1000 GPM | +-0.5 % of reading (spec 3.2), noise 0.03% of reading nominal sigma, clipped ±3 sigma [ours] |\n| P_fs, P_fr | facility pressure at HX primary inlet (after strainer) / HX primary outlet (before control valve) | 4-20 mA = 0-200 psig | noise 0.015 psi rms [ours] |\n| valve_pos | primary valve actuator position feedback | 2-10 V | |\n| spdX, kWX, faultX | per VFD over Modbus: output speed %, input power kW, fault/offline status | | |\n\n## Engineering fault catalogue ([ours])\n\n| Instrument | Failure | What the PLC reads |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| RTD / temperature transmitter | open circuit (broken lead, RTD element) | upscale burnout: >= 21 mA, i.e. 106 °C or more (NAMUR NE 43) |\n| RTD | drift (moisture in the head, element ageing) | slow offset growth, typically well under 1 °C per minute |\n| RTD / transmitter | frozen value (failed head transmitter, stale Modbus register) | constant reading, no noise |\n| RTD | damaged shield, loose terminal | large noise |\n| Flow meter | signal loop open / power lost | <= 3.6 mA, i.e. -25 GPM for the 0-1000 GPM span; model under-range sentinel -150 GPM [ours] |\n| dP transmitter | signal loop open / power lost | <= 3.6 mA, i.e. -2.5 % of span or below (NAMUR NE 43) |\n| dP transmitter | impulse line partly plugged, diaphragm drift | slow drift |\n| dP transmitter | loose terminal, EMI | large noise |\n| Flow meter | electrode fouling, empty-pipe or frozen output | frozen or offset reading |\n| Primary valve | seized stem | actuator stalls, feedback stays, command and position part |\n| Primary valve | worn packing | valve sticks, then jumps (stick-slip) |\n| Pump | worn impeller / wear rings | less head and less power at the same speed, nothing from the VFD |\n| Pump | check valve held open by debris | a stopped pump passes reverse flow and spins backwards; nothing from the VFD |\n| VFD / mains | drive trip; loss of mains | VFD fault / offline flag (Modbus) |\n\nNAMUR NE 43 (signal levels for failure information of digital transmitters): measuring information 3.8-20.5 mA; failure information <= 3.6 mA or >= 21.0 mA.\n\nThe fault catalogue describes possible equipment behavior. Only the fault scope in the work request is assessed in this release. Calibration ranges are in `/app/docs/qualification.md`.\n# the CDU pumps and hydraulics\n\n## Pump set (site-model design basis, [ours])\n\nthe CDU is a Deschutes build with the spec's 2 x 100 % pump pair replaced by **3 x 50 % pumps** (2 duty + 1 redundant, \"N+1\"), so a pump can be swapped while the CDU runs at full flow (spec 7.2). Each pump is the spec pump (SPECK TOEMA065200) hydraulics at half the flow: two pumps in parallel deliver the spec's one-pump curve (Fig 5.1.2), three exceed the spec's one-pump curve. Each pump has its own check valve on the discharge, its own VFD (ABB ACH580 class) and its own mains feed.\n\n| Item | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| Pumps | 3, in parallel, common suction (expansion tank 15 psig) and common discharge header |\n| Rated point per pump | 250 GPM at ~104 psi pump head, 100 % speed (curve fit; the spec pump nameplate says 500 GPM at 112 psi) |\n| Pump head at 100 % speed, per pump | H(q) = 120 + 0.0744 q - 5.524e-4 q^2 psi (q = GPM through that pump) |\n| Affinity | at speed n (fraction): H(q, n) = n^2 H(q/n) |\n| Common internal loss (HX secondary + filter + piping) | 5.89e-5 Q^2 psi, Q = CDU flow (8.7 psi of it is the HX, Fig 5.3.1) |\n| Available dP at the CDU flanges | pump head - internal loss: two pumps at 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2 (spec Fig 5.1.2) |\n| Brake power per pump, 100 % speed | 4.5 + 0.044 q kW (15.5 kW at 250 GPM); affinity: P(q, n) = n^3 P(q/n) |\n| Motor | 18.5 kW per pump; motor + VFD efficiency ~0.93 |\n| VFD | speed command 0-100 %; below 20 % the drive stops the pump; accel/decel ramp 10 %/s; on a drive fault or loss of mains the pump coasts down (~10 s) |\n| Check valves | one per pump, swing type, crack 0.5 psi |\n\nParallel operation note (modeled hydraulic behavior): pumps with flat curves run in parallel share flow badly when their heads differ; the weaker pump can be pushed back to shut-off behind its check valve and run without flow.\n\n## Spec curves (Deschutes v1.0 figure fits, Q in GPM, dP in psi)\n\nTwo pumps (spec pumps) running, available pressure, PG25 (Fig 5.1.1):\n\n| Speed | Fit |\n| --- | --- |\n| 100 % | 119 + 0.0211 Q - 9.34e-5 Q^2 |\n| 90 % | 94.1 + 0.0148 Q - 8.41e-5 Q^2 |\n| 80 % | 74.4 + 0.0107 Q - 7.96e-5 Q^2 |\n| 66 % | 53.2 + 0.0103 Q - 9.07e-5 Q^2 |\n| 50 % | 29.6 + 0.0112 Q - 9.88e-5 Q^2 |\n\nOne spec pump running (Fig 5.1.2), 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2.\n\nHeat exchanger secondary side dP, 2-pass (Fig 5.3.1): dP = -0.0131 + 1.41e-3 Q + 3.19e-5 Q^2 (8.7 psi at 500 GPM). The primary side has the same plates; the vendor gives the same curve for it.\n\nPrimary (facility) side across the CDU, control valve 100 % open, water (Fig 5.1.3): dP = 0.0785 + 1.58e-4 Q + 6.06e-5 Q^2 (15.3 psi at 500 GPM).\n\n## Primary control valve\n\nBelimo B6500 family, 5\" characterized control valve, equal-percentage, Cv 290 (Belimo official product-page indexed excerpt; original document unavailable, the value is retained from the client model, with this source limitation). Actuator Belimo GKRX24-MFT class, 90 s full stroke as set here, position feedback from the actuator (2-10 V). If the stem seizes the actuator stalls (feedback stays where the valve is); worn packing makes the valve stick and jump.\n# Commissioning envelope\n\nThis document describes the equipment model's parameter capabilities. The 31-case supply-control release covers the operating conditions in the work request, including cold-water and humid operation. Acceptance consists only of the six items in that request. Public commissioning cases use nominal equipment. Acceptance includes replacement equipment and faults within the following ranges. The controller receives no parameter-set identifier. Simultaneous extreme corners are not required; combined degradation and pump loss are used only where the remaining equipment can supply the requested flow.\n\n| Item | Qualification envelope | Basis and limitation |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Pump head curve | Per-pump multiplier 0.95–1.05 on the nominal head ordinate | [ours] qualification range informed by ISO 9906:2012 Table 8 grade 2B head tolerance ±5%; this is a model envelope, not a claim the installed SPECK pumps have this acceptance grade |\n| Pump capacity curve | Per-pump multiplier 0.92–1.08 on the flow abscissa | [ours] qualification range informed by ISO 9906 Table 8 grade 2B flow tolerance ±8%; independent pumps need not share exactly at equal VFD speed; ISO acceptance tolerances combine manufacturing and measurement tolerances and do not assert a probability distribution |\n| Pump efficiency | Per-pump relative efficiency 0.95–1.02 | [ours] spread around nominal, informed by ISO 9906 grade 2B efficiency tolerance −5%; the positive side is an author assumption, not an ISO upper acceptance limit |\n| Impeller condition | Equivalent-speed factor 0.90–1.00; additional relative efficiency 0.94–1.00 | [ours] reduced-order wear model: hydraulic head and capacity follow affinity scaling while wear-related leakage can increase the shaft power needed per delivered hydraulic watt. The VFD speed register still reports shaft speed |\n| Primary valve | Cv multiplier 0.95–1.05; equal-percentage rangeability 35–65, or linear / quick-opening trim | [ours] installation/trim alternatives. Fisher's handbook documents all three characteristic families; nominal Belimo trim is equal-percentage. Quick-opening is approximated by square root of fractional stem position |\n| Valve stroke | 0.9–1.1 times nominal 90 s | [ours] installation envelope; nominal actuator setting remains as in /app/docs/pumps-and-hydraulics.md (Primary valve) |\n| Valve packing | Stiction deadband 2–5% stroke, slip jump 1.2–3.5% stroke; seized stem is a separate fault | [ours] two-parameter stick-slip approximation. Choudhury et al. document the two-parameter S/J mechanism and a 2% example; the full ranges here are author-chosen and not Belimo product fault specifications |\n| Healthy Pt100 channels | Fixed calibration bias up to ±0.20 K, slow drift up to 0.012 K over a 1200 s witness test, noise 0.04–0.06 K rms | Calibration/noise/drift allocation [ours], informed by IEC 60751 class B tolerance ±(0.3 + 0.005\\|T\\|) °C quoted by WIKA. The IEC tolerance is an accuracy envelope, **not** a noise standard deviation or an ageing-rate guarantee |\n| Healthy pressure / dP channels | Calibration bias up to ±0.035 psi, drift up to 0.006 psi over a test, noise 0.015 psi rms | Allocation [ours]; Rosemount 3051C reference accuracy is ±0.065% span (range 5). At the model spans of 150/200 psi this gives ±0.0975/0.13 psi; chosen rms noise is stochastic, so those are not hard sample bounds. This is a representative industrial transmitter, not a claimed Deschutes BOM |\n| Healthy flow meter | Gain error ±0.4%, fractional noise 0.03% of reading nominal sigma clipped at ±3 sigma, zero bias/drift | Allocation [ours] enforces the Deschutes ±0.5% reading specification: worst combined gain and noise is 0.4% + 1.004 × 0.09% = 0.49036% of reading. The percentage allocation and clipping are model choices; the source accuracy is not a Gaussian sigma. The noise scales with actual flow and vanishes at zero flow |\n| Training load | B200 Llama-8B and B200 diffusion training traces; coherent training shapes and time-shifted trace segments | Published measured node power; converting node watts to rack liquid heat and phase-aligning groups are [ours], represented by the supplied commissioning simulations |\n\nLow head/flow, wear and loss of a pump are combined only when the remaining equipment can still provide the requested flow with sensible control. Equipment spread changes the readings and hydraulic behavior used for control. The wider equipment model supports faults on either redundant channel and recurring faults; the complete fault scope is specified in the work request. Calibration spread and legitimate rack service remain healthy conditions.\n\n\n## Implemented model variations\n\nThe pump tables are actually changed: `flow_rate_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_flow`, `head_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_head`, and power is scaled by head × flow / efficiency. The same power scaling is applied to VFD kW telemetry. Wear acts on the hydraulic effective speed and separately on efficiency; it changes the physical pump behavior.\n\nThe facility valve remains a physical valve. Its effective opening is mapped to the required Cv characteristic, while `valve_pos` reports actual stem position. Stiction and seizure act on that stem position, before the characteristic. Bias, gain, seeded noise and slow drift act on instrument signals independently of fault injection. Fault mode 6 is a recurring 10 s invalid burst every 60 s; it recovers between bursts.\n\n\n## Site operating range\n\nThe row has four rack groups, each designed for 125 GPM. The acceptance runs use facility supply water from 17 to 30 °C, room dew point from 12 to 21 °C, load-bank and training workloads up to 2 MW, and partial-row service with deliberate rack-group isolation and restoration. These ranges describe the supplied tests; they do not imply every cross-product corner is required. This release uses water and 25 psid facility differential pressure; PG25 is not part of this 31-case qualification scope.\n## Vendor practice (published CDU controller manuals; for reference, not the CDU's sequence)\n\nVertiv CoolChip CDU070 Operation and Maintenance Guide (SL-80016, 2025):\n\n- Three supply temperature sensors T3a/b/c: \"The controller takes an average between all 3 readings as its input value.\" A sensor more than 1 °C from the others raises a \"Diff. Out of Limits\" alarm \"after a default 30 second delay\" and the controller then averages the two remaining sensors. An open-circuit sensor raises its \"Sensor Fault\" alarm \"with no time delay\".\n- Pressure sensor fault: reading outside the normal range; \"For DP control, if system differential pressure is not valid, then pump speed will remain at last known demand.\"\n- \"All sensors related to control (PS1, PS2 and T2) are redundant ... so a single sensor failure will not impact the operation or the status of the CoolChip CDU070.\"\n- Run/standby pumps: \"If Pump 1 fails to reach 90% (default) of the DP/flow demand in the default time period of 100 seconds, it is assumed there is a pump flow/pressure fault. The pump ramps down to a stop. Pump 2 is initialized.\" Duty rotation weekly; changeover takes about 0.25 s (P210 default 250 ms).\n- Low flow / low DP alarm at 80 % of set point after 30 s (P204-P206). Over-pressure alarm set point P211. Secondary temperature alarms 2 °C below/above set point (P310/P311).\n- Group control: standby pumps \"start in 75 ms and a 2 second ramp up ... to seamlessly maintain system differential pressure if a unit is lost without over/undershoots\"; a failure speed offset (P217) is applied \"to avoid spikes in differential pressure\"; the 2 s VFD ramp \"is designed to prevent a secondary discharge pressure over shoot on ... (pump) restart\".\n- After a power failure the unit restarts (P703 \"Post Power Failure Options\": Run).\n\nLenovo Neptune RM100 in-rack CDU user guide: low-flow alarm at 90 % of the flow set point after 100 s (P204, P206); pump failover when the running pump does not reach 90 % of demand within 100 s; secondary temperature alarms 2 °C from set point; dew-point offset 3 °C.\n\nOCP Liquid-to-Liquid CDU Test Methodology r1, 3.2.4: \"there will be system alarms and notifications communicated to the operator if the actual flow conditions deviate from preset values or when abnormal conditions occur.\"\n\nThe nominal noise above is for the released commissioning plant; qualification calibration, noise and drift envelopes are in qualification.md. The fault catalogue is an author-created witness-test list, not an observed vendor fleet history.\n\nCross-channel plausibility checking of redundant channels — the two supply RTDs, the two dP transmitters — is standard commissioning practice, consistent with the published sequences above: keep an agreement window with a short persistence delay, and when one channel departs, exclude it and carry the healthy channel. A supervisory release for this row is expected to know this; the instrument set deliberately ships the redundant pairs to support it.\n# Acceptance bounds and validation\n\n## What the bounds mean\n\nThis task checks a declared operating envelope, not numerical agreement with the\nreference controller. Different algorithms may issue different commands and\nproduce different trajectories. A solution is correct when every assessed sample\nremains within the stated service limits. No reference trajectory or controller\noutput is used as the expected answer.\n\n| Bound | Engineering basis and benchmark allocation |\n|---|---|\n| 125 GPM per rack group | Four groups partition the 500 GPM nominal secondary flow of OCP Project Deschutes v1.0, section 3.1. The four-group split is the author's plant configuration. |\n| At least 90% design flow | The commissioning low-flow service floor permits a 10% reduction from nominal flow. It is an author-selected task requirement, not a tolerance fitted to a controller's output. |\n| Supply at least dew point + 2 K | Condensation protection margin; the benchmark applies it to physical plant truth rather than noisy controller measurements. Instrument accuracy and noise are separately defined in `environment/work/docs/qualification.md`. |\n| Supply at most 55 C | The Deschutes liquid operating-temperature upper bound (v1.0 sections 3.1 and 5). |\n| Supply at most 120 psig | Author-selected site alarm limit, 10 psi below the 130 psig product relief setting (Deschutes section 3.2). |\n| Differential pressure at most 95 psid | Author-selected transient ceiling, 5 psi above the 80–90 psi nominal product range; the normal control target is 85 psid. |\n| Less than 30 consecutive seconds below 50% design flow | Author-selected loss-of-service criterion for this simulated rack. It is a separate prolonged-starvation guard, not a claim that every real GPU trips at exactly 30 seconds. The 90% floor is stricter in this release; the starvation item remains explicit in the contract and report. |\n\nProduct source: OCP Project Deschutes CDU Specification v1.0, 26 February 2026,\nhttps://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-specification-deschutes-v1-0-pdf.\nThe table distinguishes product facts from benchmark/site choices. These bounds\ndefine the task before scoring; successful controllers establish feasibility\nwithin this envelope, not the origin of the engineering requirements.\n\n## Numerical and algorithm variation\n\nThe verifier evaluates physical truth on the fixed one-second log after 300 seconds\nof startup. Sensor bias and noise affect the controller observations, not the truth\nused for scoring. The healthy replacement and calibration ranges are specified in\n`environment/work/docs/qualification.md`; fault combinations are bounded by the\npublished task scope. The same power scaling is applied to VFD kW telemetry. Wear acts on the hydraulic effective speed and separately on efficiency; it changes the physical pump behavior.\n\nThe facility valve remains a physical valve. Sensor bias and noise affect the controller observations, not the truth\nused for scoring. The healthy replacement and calibration ranges are specified in\n`environment/work/docs/qualification.md`; fault combinations are bounded by the\npublished task scope. The runtime fixes the 0.5-second control scan and one-scan\ncommand delay for all implementations.\n\nThe first five reported metrics are sums of **positive violations**, so zero means\nno sample crossed a service limit. Zero is not a requirement that a computed\ntrajectory equal a reference trajectory to floating-point precision. Values on the\nallowed side can differ freely. The comparison does not hide an epsilon around\nthe physical limits; controllers should retain operational margin. The isolated\nsampling-timestamp check uses a separate 1e-8-second tolerance and is not a physical\nacceptance allowance. Boundary probes in `boundary-probes.json` exercise exact\nlimits and crossings using the actual metric implementation.\n\n## Validation with independent controllers\n\n`independent-controller-results.json` contains the complete six-metric matrix for\neach case in five frozen native model submissions, including distinct candidate\nhashes and hashes of their original reports. Two independently generated\ncontrollers (normal03 and normal05) passed every case; three others failed physical\nrequirements. This supports algorithm-independent feasibility and rejection of\nactual incorrect implementations. It is not a new difficulty measurement of the\nrevised instruction, and it does not claim a general numerical sensitivity study\nor robustness beyond the stated scenario envelope.\n\nThe boundary probes complement those native runs: they verify that equality is\naccepted at each inclusive bound, small crossings are rejected, out-of-service\nracks are excluded, and 29 versus 30 consecutive low-flow samples are distinguished.\nThese are deterministic metric tests, not substitute FMU or model runs.
